````math
Equations [30](#A2.E30 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Proposition . ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
[31](#A2.E31 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Proposition . ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
and [33](#A2.E33 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Proposition . ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
verify
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
∎

#### Scope of the graph conditions.

The support conditions above give a concrete finite-parameter
realization of
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
However, our learning and forgetting results apply more broadly under
their respective assumptions.
Equation [25](#A2.E25 "In item 1 ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
ensures normalization when inter-cluster transitions are rare.
Equation [26](#A2.E26 "In item 2 ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
simplifies the construction by ensuring that the first boundary is
reached in one step. More generally, the within-cluster routing
conditions allow multiple steps, provided boundary hitting is almost
surely finite and the bounds on invalid-boundary probability and
valid-boundary coverage in
Equations [4](#S2.E4 "In Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [5](#S2.E5 "In Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
hold uniformly over relevant starts.

### B.2 Gradient of the SD loss

###### Lemma 4 (Gradient of the SD loss).

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}L_{\mathrm{SD}}(\mathbf{W};\mathcal{D}_{\ell})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{i=1}^{I^{(b)}}\mathbf{1}\{\tau_{i}^{(b)}<T^{(b)}\}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v_{i}^{(b)}\mid u_{i}^{(b)},x_{\ell}^{(b)})`$ |  | (38) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\cdot x_{\ell}^{(b)}\left(e_{u_{i}^{(b)},v_{i}^{(b)}}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u_{i}^{(b)})}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u_{i}^{(b)},x_{\ell}^{(b)})e_{u_{i}^{(b)},v}\right)^{\top}`$ |  | (39) |

#### Sketch of proof of Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Gradient of the SD loss). ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

- •
  Differentiate the one-state stop-gradient KL and show that
  successor-independent teacher factors contribute zero gradient
  (Lemma [5](#Thmlemma5 "Lemma 5 (The one-state stop-gradient identity). ‣ Sketch of proof of Lemma . ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Identify the active hint factor and show that the local gradient
  vanishes away from hint-source states
  (Lemma [6](#Thmlemma6 "Lemma 6 (Cancellation of inactive hints). ‣ Sketch of proof of Lemma . ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Sum the local gradients over visited states and average over the
  mini-batch to obtain the stated sparse gradient formula
  (Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Gradient of the SD loss). ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).

###### Lemma 5 (The one-state stop-gradient identity).

For $`p_{t}(v)=\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid y_{t},x_{\ell})`$ and the
stop-gradient teacher $`q_{t}`$, the KL and softmax derivatives satisfy
Equation [42](#A2.E42 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Lemma . ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")–Equation [43](#A2.E43 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Lemma . ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
In particular, a teacher factor independent of the proposed successor
contributes zero gradient.

###### Proof.

For one batch element and one time $`t<T`$, set

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle u:=y_{t},\quad h_{t}:=(y_{0},\ldots,y_{t}),\quad p_{t}(v):=\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell}),\quad q_{t}(v):=\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid h_{t},x_{\ell},c).`$ |  |

With

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{W}}(c\mid h_{t},v,x_{\ell}):=\prod_{i=1}^{I}\chi_{\mathbf{W}}(c_{i}\mid h_{t},v,x_{\ell}),
``` |  | (40) |
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle q_{t}(v)=\frac{\chi_{\mathbf{W}}(c\mid h_{t},v,x_{\ell})p_{t}(v)}{\sum_{z\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\chi_{\mathbf{W}}(c\mid h_{t},z,x_{\ell})p_{t}(z)},
``` |  | (41) |

we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{t}\parallel\operatorname{SG}(q_{t}))`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}p_{t}(v)\log\frac{p_{t}(v)}{q_{t}(v)},`$ |  | (42) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}p_{t}(v)`$ | $`\displaystyle=p_{t}(v)x_{\ell}\left(e_{u,v}-\sum_{z\in\mathcal{N}(u)}p_{t}(z)e_{u,z}\right)^{\top},`$ |  | (43) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}p_{t}(v)`$ | $`\displaystyle=0.`$ |  | (44) |

∎

###### Lemma 6 (Cancellation of inactive hints).

With the hint in
Equation [9](#S3.E9 "In 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
the one-state gradient vanishes unless $`t=\tau_{i}<T`$ for a hint edge
$`(u_{i},v_{i})`$. At such a time it equals
$`\log(\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v_{i}\mid u_{i},x_{\ell})`$.

###### Proof.

By
Lemma [5](#Thmlemma5 "Lemma 5 (The one-state stop-gradient identity). ‣ Sketch of proof of Lemma . ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
it suffices to identify the successor-dependent hint factor. For each
hint edge $`c_{i}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{W}}(c_{i}\mid h_{t},v,x_{\ell})\propto\begin{cases}1,&\tau_{i}=t,\ v=v_{i},\\
\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}},&\tau_{i}=t,\ v\neq v_{i},\\
1,&\tau_{i}<t,\ y_{\tau_{i}+1}=v_{i},\\
\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}},&\tau_{i}<t,\ y_{\tau_{i}+1}\neq v_{i},\\
Z_{\mathbf{W},i}(x_{\ell}),&\tau_{i}>t,\end{cases}`$ |  |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle Z_{\mathbf{W},i}(x_{\ell})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v_{i}\mid u_{i},x_{\ell})+\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}\left(1-\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v_{i}\mid u_{i},x_{\ell})\right).`$ |  |

Hence, if

|     |                                                        |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     | $`\displaystyle t\notin\{\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{I}\},`$ |     |

then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{W}}(c\mid h_{t},v,x_{\ell})`$ | $`\displaystyle\equiv\text{const. for }v\quad\Longrightarrow\quad q_{t}(v)=p_{t}(v)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{t}\parallel\operatorname{SG}(q_{t}))=0.`$ |  |

If $`t=\tau_{i}<T`$, node-simplicity of the sampled teacher path gives
$`\tau_{i^{\prime}}\neq t`$ for $`i^{\prime}\neq i`$, and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle Z_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=p_{t}(v_{i})+\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}(1-p_{t}(v_{i})),`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle q_{t}(v)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\begin{cases}p_{t}(v)/Z_{t},&v=v_{i},\\
\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}p_{t}(v)/Z_{t},&v\neq v_{i}.\end{cases}`$ |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{t}\parallel\operatorname{SG}(q_{t}))`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v}\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}p_{t}(v)\log Z_{t}-(\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\sum_{v\neq v_{i}}\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}p_{t}(v)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=(\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}p_{t}(v_{i})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=(\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})p_{t}(v_{i})x_{\ell}\left(e_{u_{i},v_{i}}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u_{i})}p_{t}(v)e_{u_{i},v}\right)^{\top}.`$ |  |

∎

#### Main proof of Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Gradient of the SD loss). ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

###### Proof.

Apply
Lemma [6](#Thmlemma6 "Lemma 6 (Cancellation of inactive hints). ‣ Sketch of proof of Lemma . ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
to each visited state. Node simplicity permits each hint source to
contribute at most once. Averaging over the $`B`$ paths gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}L_{\mathrm{SD}}(\mathbf{W};\mathcal{D}_{\ell})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{i=1}^{I^{(b)}}\mathbf{1}\{\tau_{i}^{(b)}<T^{(b)}\}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v_{i}^{(b)}\mid u_{i}^{(b)},x_{\ell}^{(b)})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad\cdot x_{\ell}^{(b)}\left(e_{u_{i}^{(b)},v_{i}^{(b)}}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u_{i}^{(b)})}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u_{i}^{(b)},x_{\ell}^{(b)})e_{u_{i}^{(b)},v}\right)^{\top}.`$ |  |

∎

### B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions

We introduce the notation required for the following statement. Fix a
task $`\mathcal{D}_{\ell}`$, and consider $`n`$ consecutive SD updates
on this task starting from $`\mathbf{W}^{(0)}`$. Let
$`N_{\ell}\mathrel{:=}|E_{\ell}|`$, and enumerate the elements of
$`E_{\ell}`$ as $`e_{m}=(u_{m},v_{m})`$, $`m\in\{1,\ldots,N_{\ell}\}`$.
Assumption [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and the definition of $`M`$ imply
$`N_{\ell}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\partial_{\ell}^{\star}C_{k}|\leq KM`$.

Let $`\mathcal{S}_{\ell,k}\subseteq\mathring{C}_{k}`$ be the set of
relevant starts in $`C_{k}`$: the task start $`y_{\ell,0}`$ when it
belongs to $`C_{k}`$, an endpoint in $`C_{k}`$ of a correct
inter-cluster transition entering $`C_{k}`$. For
$`s\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell,k}`$, write $`U_{\ell,k}^{s}`$ for the first
boundary node reached by the rollout started from $`s`$, and define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\lambda_{\ell}\mathrel{:=}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}k:\,\partial_{\ell}^{\star}C_{k}\neq\emptyset\\
s\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell,k},\,u\in\partial_{\ell}^{\star}C_{k}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{W}^{(0)}}\left(U_{\ell,k}^{s}=u\mid s,x_{\ell}\right).`$ |  | (45) |

Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
implies the explicit, $`K`$-uniform bound
$`\lambda_{\ell}\geq c_{\mathrm{route}}/M>0`$.
Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Gradient of the SD loss). ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
shows that SD updates only edges whose sources are hint-source boundary
nodes. Hence all transitions whose sources are interior nodes remain
unchanged, and the first-boundary distribution in
Equation [45](#A2.E45 "In B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
is preserved during SD training.

For each $`e_{m}`$, fix a shortest sequence
$`\mathcal{Q}_{m}=(C_{k_{0}},\ldots,C_{k_{L_{m}}})`$ in the
cluster-level graph induced by $`E_{\ell}`$, from the cluster containing
$`y_{\ell,0}`$ to the cluster containing $`u_{m}`$. The sequence can be
chosen without repeated clusters, and therefore $`L_{m}\leq K-1`$. By
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
every correct inter-cluster edge has pretrained probability at least
$`c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2}`$. We therefore use the $`K`$-explicit
lower bound

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\underline{\rho}_{\ell,K,M}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}\left(\frac{c_{\mathrm{route}}}{M}\right)^{K}(c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2})^{K-1}=\frac{c_{\mathrm{route}}^{K}c_{\mathrm{int}}^{K-1}\epsilon_{2}^{K-1}}{M^{K}|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}.`$ |  | (46) |

Under
Equation [6](#S2.E6 "In Fine-tuning settings ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
every path in $`\mathcal{P}_{\ell}`$ is sampled with probability exactly
$`1/|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|`$. The conditional independence properties used
below are exactly those specified in the fine-tuning setup above. Let
$`\{\eta_{j}\}_{j\geq 1}`$ be a diminishing positive step-size sequence,
and let $`\delta\in(0,1)`$. Define the finite-sample effective drift by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)`$ | $`\displaystyle:={}\left[\underline{\rho}_{\ell,K,M}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}-\sqrt{\frac{\log(KM/\delta)}{2B}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}^{2}}\right]^{+}.`$ |  | (47) |

###### Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions).

Suppose
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
hold and $`0<\epsilon_{2}\leq 1`$. With probability at least
$`1-\delta`$, simultaneously for every $`(u_{m},v_{m})\in E_{\ell}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(n)}}(v_{m}\mid u_{m},x_{\ell})\geq 1-O\!\left(\left[\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{2}^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}}\right)\mathfrak{D}_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)\right]^{-1}\right),`$ |  | (48) |

where $`[a]_{+}:=\max\{a,0\}`$. If in addition
$`\eta_{j}=\Theta(j^{-\nu})`$ for some $`\nu\in[0,1)`$, then for
sufficiently large $`n`$ the drift term dominates the fluctuation term
and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(n)}}(v_{m}\mid u_{m},x_{\ell})\geq 1-O\!\left(\left[\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{2}^{2}\left(\log\frac{1}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}}\right)\underline{\rho}_{\ell,K,M}n^{1-\nu}\right]^{-1}\right).`$ |  | (49) |

The learning term inside the brackets has the explicit $`M,K`$
dependence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}^{2}\underline{\rho}_{\ell,K,M}n^{1-\nu}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{c_{\mathrm{route}}^{K}c_{\mathrm{int}}^{K-1}}{M^{K}|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}\epsilon_{2}^{K+1}n^{1-\nu}.`$ |  |

#### Sketch of proof of Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions). ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

- •
  Show that local SD updates increase correct logit gaps and preserve
  the interior transition kernel
  (Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Local update and invariant interior transitions). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Use retained boundary coverage to lower-bound the probability of an
  update opportunity for each correct edge
  (Lemma [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Control the weighted update counts simultaneously using conditional
  mini-batch concentration
  (Lemma [10](#Thmlemma10 "Lemma 10 (Simultaneous learning with finite mini-batches). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Convert the weighted update counts into transition-error bounds
  through the odds estimate
  (Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Error from weighted update counts). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).

#### Shared notation and constants.

The local update and odds estimates below apply under either
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
or
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Existence of a rare entrance). ‣ 3.3 A theoretical implication for pre-training diversity ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
Unless stated otherwise, the SD lemmas concern training on the fixed
task alone. Throughout the SD lemmas, $`C_{\mathrm{bad}}\epsilon_{1}`$
bounds the initial invalid-boundary probability and
$`c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2}`$ bounds every initial correct
inter-cluster probability from below. The constants are positive and
uniform; we may take $`c_{\mathrm{int}}\leq 1`$ and
$`c_{\mathrm{route}}/M\leq 1`$. For the rare-entrance case, let
$`e_{m}=(u_{m},v_{m})`$ be the edge in
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Existence of a rare entrance). ‣ 3.3 A theoretical implication for pre-training diversity ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
so $`u_{m}=u^{*}`$. The first-boundary lower bound is retained at all
other correct boundaries; $`c_{\mathrm{route}}`$ and
$`\underline{\rho}_{\ell,K,M}`$ use only this retained lower bound in
the rare case.

Let $`\mathcal{F}_{j-1}`$ be the training history before iteration
$`j`$. Define

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\tau_{i}^{(b,j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\inf\{t\geq 0:y_{t}^{(b,j)}=u_{i}\},`$ | $`\displaystyle I_{i}^{(b,j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\{e_{i}\in c^{(b,j)}\}\mathbf{1}\{\tau_{i}^{(b,j)}<T^{(b,j)}\},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{b=1}^{B}I_{i}^{(b,j)},`$ | $`\displaystyle p_{i}^{(j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=p_{u_{i},v_{i}}^{(j)},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle p_{u,v}^{(j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(j)}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell}),`$ | $`\displaystyle z_{u,v}^{(j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=x_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{(j)}e_{u,v},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle r_{i}^{(j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle=(1-p_{i}^{(j)})/p_{i}^{(j)},`$ | $`\displaystyle a_{i,j}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{j}}{B}\log(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}p_{i}^{(j-1)}.`$ |  |

The symbols $`e_{u,v}`$ denote one-hot edge features; $`e_{i}`$ denotes
a graph edge.
Assumption [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives distinct sources $`u_{i}`$ and
$`N_{\ell}=\sum_{h}|\partial_{\ell}^{\star}C_{h}|\leq KM`$.

###### Lemma 7 (Local update and invariant interior transitions).

For every training history and every outgoing edge of $`u_{i}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{W}^{(j)}e_{u_{i},v}=\mathbf{W}^{(j-1)}e_{u_{i},v}+a_{i,j}(\mathbf{1}\{v=v_{i}\}-p_{u_{i},v}^{(j-1)})x_{\ell}.
``` |  | (50) |

Only columns whose sources occur in $`E_{\ell}`$ can change. Moreover,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
p_{i}^{(j)}\geq p_{i}^{(j-1)},\qquad\frac{p_{u_{i},v}^{(j)}}{p_{i}^{(j)}}\leq\frac{p_{u_{i},v}^{(j-1)}}{p_{i}^{(j-1)}}.
``` |  | (51) |

First-boundary hitting distributions from relevant starts remain equal
to their initial values.

###### Proof.

Lemma [4](#Thmlemma4 "Lemma 4 (Gradient of the SD loss). ‣ B.2 Gradient of the SD loss ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives the visited-source contribution

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(\log\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})p_{i}^{(j-1)}x_{\ell}\left(e_{u_{i},v_{i}}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u_{i})}p_{u_{i},v}^{(j-1)}e_{u_{i},v}\right)^{\top}.
``` |  |

The hint’s normalization term vanishes because
$`\sum_{v}\nabla p_{u_{i},v}=0`$. At other sources the hinted and
unhinted distributions agree, giving zero gradient. Uniqueness, node
simplicity, and mini-batch averaging yield
Equation [50](#A2.E50 "In Lemma 7 (Local update and invariant interior transitions). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
For $`v\neq v_{i}`$, the correct-minus-incorrect logit gap increases by

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{i,j}\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}(1-p_{i}^{(j-1)}+p_{u_{i},v}^{(j-1)})\geq 0.
``` |  |

Exponentiating proves the ratio inequality, and summing the
incorrect-to-correct ratios proves monotonicity of $`p_{i}^{(j)}`$.
Interior sources never occur in hints, so all transitions before the
first boundary are unchanged. This also preserves boundary-hitting
properness. ∎

###### Lemma 8 (Error from weighted update counts).

For every history and every $`i`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-p_{i}^{(n)}\leq\left[1+c_{\mathrm{int}}^{2}\epsilon_{2}^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{-1}.
``` |  | (52) |

If $`p_{i}^{(0)}<1`$, the stronger estimate is

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
r_{i}^{(n)}\leq\left[(r_{i}^{(0)})^{-1}+\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}(p_{i}^{(0)})^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}\right]^{-1}.
``` |  | (53) |

###### Proof.

If $`p_{i}^{(0)}=1`$, the assertion is immediate. Otherwise the
logit-gap estimate above gives
$`r_{i}^{(j)}\leq r_{i}^{(j-1)}\exp[-a_{i,j}\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}(1-p_{i}^{(j-1)})]`$.
Since $`r_{i}^{(j-1)}\leq r_{i}^{(0)}`$ and
$`p_{i}^{(j-1)}(1-p_{i}^{(j-1)})\geq r_{i}^{(j-1)}/(1+r_{i}^{(0)})^{2}`$,
the inequality $`e^{t}\geq 1+t`$ implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{r_{i}^{(j)}}\geq\frac{1}{r_{i}^{(j-1)}}+\frac{\eta_{j}}{B}\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}(p_{i}^{(0)})^{2}\log(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}.
``` |  |

Telescoping proves
Equation [53](#A2.E53 "In Lemma 8 (Error from weighted update counts). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
For the error bound, use
$`p_{i}^{(0)}\geq c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2}`$ and
$`\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 1`$, and write
$`1-p_{i}^{(n)}=(1+1/r_{i}^{(n)})^{-1}`$. ∎

###### Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage).

Under
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
every $`e_{i}\in E_{\ell}`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[I_{i}^{(b,j)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]\geq\underline{\rho}_{\ell,K,M}.
``` |  | (54) |

Under
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Existence of a rare entrance). ‣ 3.3 A theoretical implication for pre-training diversity ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
the same holds for every $`e_{i}\neq e_{m}`$ whose source cluster is
reachable from the original-start cluster in the cluster graph induced
by $`E_{\ell}\setminus\{e_{m}\}`$.

###### Proof.

Fix an edge in the specified set. Choose a shortest sequence of clusters
leading to its source cluster, using correct inter-cluster edges
(excluding $`e_{m}`$ in the second case). The sequence has no repeated
cluster and at most $`K-1`$ transitions. Specify one such edge between
each adjacent pair, and finally specify boundary $`u_{i}`$ in the last
cluster. In the second case all specified boundary sources differ from
$`u_{m}`$, since its unique correct edge is $`e_{m}`$. Also, the
sequence cannot enter $`C_{k}`$ by
Assumption [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Existence of a rare entrance). ‣ 3.3 A theoretical implication for pre-training diversity ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

Every internal segment begins at a relevant start. Its probability of
first reaching the specified boundary is at least
$`c_{\mathrm{route}}/M`$, by retained coverage and
Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Local update and invariant interior transitions). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
At each required crossing the correct edge probability is at least
$`c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2}`$, by monotonicity. Apply the Markov
property successively at these boundary and entry times. There are at
most $`K`$ internal segments and $`K-1`$ crossings, so

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}(\tau_{i}^{(b,j)}<T^{(b,j)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1})\geq(c_{\mathrm{route}}/M)^{K}(c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2})^{K-1}.
``` |  |

Both lower-bound factors can be taken at most one. Since
$`e_{i}\in E_{\ell}`$, at least one correct path contains $`e_{i}`$, so
the uniform hint contains it with probability at least
$`1/|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|`$. The stipulated conditional independence of
hint and student gives
Equation [54](#A2.E54 "In Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
∎

###### Lemma 10 (Simultaneous learning with finite mini-batches).

For the applicable edge set in
Lemma [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
with probability at least $`1-\delta`$, every such edge satisfies

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}\geq\mathfrak{D}_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta).
``` |  | (55) |

Combining this event with
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Error from weighted update counts). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives simultaneous error bounds for all these edges.

###### Proof.

For a fixed $`i`$, conditional mini-batch independence and Hoeffding’s
lemma give, for $`t>0`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\exp\!\left\{-\frac{t\eta_{j}}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}(I_{i}^{(b,j)}-\mathbb{E}[I_{i}^{(b,j)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}])\right\}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\leq\exp\!\left(\frac{t^{2}\eta_{j}^{2}}{8B}\right).`$ |  |

Iterating this bound over $`j`$ gives the exponent
$`t^{2}\sum_{j}\eta_{j}^{2}/(8B)`$ for the full sum. Exponential Markov
and optimization over $`t`$ imply

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\!\left(\frac{1}{B}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}\sum_{b=1}^{B}[I_{i}^{(b,j)}-\mathbb{E}[I_{i}^{(b,j)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]]<-\sqrt{\frac{\log(KM/\delta)}{2B}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{KM}.`$ |  |

Use
Lemma [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and union-bound over at most $`KM`$ edges. Nonnegativity of the weighted
counts permits taking the positive part, giving
Equation [55](#A2.E55 "In Lemma 10 (Simultaneous learning with finite mini-batches). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Error from weighted update counts). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives the error bound. If the edge set is empty, the simultaneous
assertion is vacuous. ∎

###### Lemma 11 (Converting edge accuracy to rollout accuracy).

Under
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2 (Pre-trained diversity). ‣ Pre-training requirements ‣ 2.2 Pre-training and fine-tuning ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
consider any predictor whose interior kernel equals the pretrained
kernel, and denote its edge probabilities by $`p_{i}^{(n)}`$. Then every
relevant start $`s\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell,h}`$ satisfies

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle 1-\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(n)}}(y_{T}\in\tilde{C}_{\ell}\mid y_{0}=s,x_{\ell})\leq C_{\mathrm{bad}}(1+N_{\ell})\epsilon_{1}+\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\ell}}(1-p_{i}^{(n)}).`$ |  | (56) |

Under
Assumptions [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1 (transition uniqueness and competition between tasks). ‣ Continual learning. ‣ 2.1 Continual learning on CoT graph ‣ 2 Problem Setup ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [3](#Thmassumption3 "Assumption 3 (Existence of a rare entrance). ‣ 3.3 A theoretical implication for pre-training diversity ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
if the edges specified in
Lemma [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
have error at most $`\epsilon_{1}`$, then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle 1-\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(n)}}(y_{T}\in\tilde{C}_{\ell}\mid y_{\ell,0},x_{\ell})`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\mathrm{bad}}(1+KM)\epsilon_{1}+KM\epsilon_{1}+\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(n)}}(\exists t<T:y_{t}=u_{m}\mid y_{\ell,0},x_{\ell}).`$ |  | (57) |

The first inequality is deterministic and does not require the predictor
to have been trained on the fixed task alone.

###### Proof.

For
Equation [56](#A2.E56 "In Lemma 11 (Converting edge accuracy to rollout accuracy). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
follow a rollout until reaching the target or the first invalid boundary
or incorrect choice at a valid boundary. Before these events, each
internal segment starts at a relevant start and is followed by a correct
inter-cluster edge. Conditional on the history up to each segment’s
start, the invalid-boundary probability is at most
$`C_{\mathrm{bad}}\epsilon_{1}`$. By node simplicity there are at most
$`1+N_{\ell}`$ segments and each correct source is visited at most once.
Summing conditional probabilities, including the events that the
segments actually start, gives respectively
$`C_{\mathrm{bad}}(1+N_{\ell})\epsilon_{1}`$ and
$`\sum_{i}(1-p_{i}^{(n)})`$. An incorrect choice includes choosing an
intra-cluster edge at a valid boundary.

These events cover failure: any reached nonterminal cluster has a
correct exit, because the arriving correct edge lies on a correct path
with a remaining suffix. The analogous statement holds for the original
start. Properness makes each internal segment finite. If the target were
never reached while both errors were avoided, one would traverse more
than $`N_{\ell}`$ distinct correct sources, contradicting node
simplicity. For a start already in the target the conclusion is
immediate.

For
Equation [57](#A2.E57 "In Lemma 11 (Converting edge accuracy to rollout accuracy). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
start from $`y_{\ell,0}`$ and stop additionally upon visiting $`u_{m}`$.
Before that visit every current cluster is reachable via
$`E_{\ell}\setminus\{e_{m}\}`$, so each encountered correct boundary
other than $`u_{m}`$ belongs to the learned set in
Lemma [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
Its choice error is at most $`\epsilon_{1}`$. The invalid-boundary term
is unchanged, and the extra stopping event is bounded by the
unconditional evaluation visitation probability displayed in
Equation [57](#A2.E57 "In Lemma 11 (Converting edge accuracy to rollout accuracy). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
The same properness argument covers failure. ∎

#### Main proof of Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions). ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

###### Proof.

Lemmas [9](#Thmlemma9 "Lemma 9 (Update opportunities from retained coverage). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [10](#Thmlemma10 "Lemma 10 (Simultaneous learning with finite mini-batches). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
give
$`B^{-1}\sum_{j}\eta_{j}\mathfrak{n}_{i}^{(j)}\geq\mathfrak{D}_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)`$
simultaneously for all $`i`$, with probability at least $`1-\delta`$. If
$`p_{i}^{(0)}=1`$, the transition is already correct with probability
one. Otherwise,
$`(r_{i}^{(0)})^{-1}=p_{i}^{(0)}/(1-p_{i}^{(0)})\geq c_{\mathrm{int}}\epsilon_{2}`$
and $`(p_{i}^{(0)})^{2}\geq c_{\mathrm{int}}^{2}\epsilon_{2}^{2}`$. The
stronger estimate in
Lemma [8](#Thmlemma8 "Lemma 8 (Error from weighted update counts). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
together with $`1-p_{i}^{(n)}\leq r_{i}^{(n)}`$, proves
Equation [48](#A2.E48 "In Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions). ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
For fixed $`0\leq\nu<1`$, $`\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}=\Theta(n^{1-\nu})`$,
whereas $`(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\eta_{j}^{2})^{1/2}`$ is respectively
$`O(n^{1/2-\nu})`$, $`O(\sqrt{\log(en)})`$, or $`O(1)`$ as $`\nu<1/2`$,
$`\nu=1/2`$, or $`\nu>1/2`$. Thus the fluctuation is smaller than the
drift for sufficiently large $`n`$, which proves
Equation [49](#A2.E49 "In Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions). ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
∎

### B.4 Single-task rollout accuracy under SD

See
[1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Single-task learning under SD). ‣ SD as on-policy training ‣ 3.1 Single-task learnability ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")

#### Sketch of proof of Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Single-task learning under SD). ‣ SD as on-policy training ‣ 3.1 Single-task learnability ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

- •
  Bound the errors of all correct inter-cluster transitions on one
  common training event
  (Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions). ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Show that the interior transition kernel retains its pre-trained
  routing guarantees
  (Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Local update and invariant interior transitions). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Bound rollout failure by the probabilities of invalid-boundary
  encounters and incorrect transition choices
  (Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11 (Converting edge accuracy to rollout accuracy). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).

#### Main proof of Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Single-task learning under SD). ‣ SD as on-policy training ‣ 3.1 Single-task learnability ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

###### Proof.

Proposition [2](#Thmproposition2 "Proposition 2 (Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions). ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives an event of probability at least $`1-\delta`$ on which
$`1-p_{i}^{(n)}\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\xi_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)`$ for
every $`i`$.
Lemma [7](#Thmlemma7 "Lemma 7 (Local update and invariant interior transitions). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
preserves the interior kernel. Apply
Lemma [11](#Thmlemma11 "Lemma 11 (Converting edge accuracy to rollout accuracy). ‣ Shared notation and constants. ‣ B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
with $`s=y_{\ell,0}`$ to obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 1-\pi_{\mathbf{W}^{(n)}}(y_{T}\in\tilde{C}_{\ell}\mid y_{\ell,0},x_{\ell})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{bad}}(1+N_{\ell})\epsilon_{1}+N_{\ell}C_{\mathrm{tr}}\xi_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=O(KM\epsilon_{1})+O(KM\xi_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)).`$ |  |

This proves
Equation [12](#S3.E12 "In Theorem 1 (Single-task learning under SD). ‣ SD as on-policy training ‣ 3.1 Single-task learnability ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
on that same event. In the regime where the learning term dominates,
substitution of
Equation [46](#A2.E46 "In B.3 Learning hint-supported inter-cluster transitions ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
KM\xi_{\ell,n}^{(K)}(\delta)=O\!\left(\frac{KM^{K+1}|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}{c_{\mathrm{route}}^{K}c_{\mathrm{int}}^{K-1}\epsilon_{2}^{K+1}n^{1-\nu}\log(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}})}\right).
``` |  |

∎

### B.5 Single-task learning under SFT

Set

|     |                           |                                       |     |
|-----|---------------------------|---------------------------------------|-----|
|     | $`\displaystyle m_{\nu}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\min\{\nu,1-\nu\}.`$ |     |

We prove the stronger batch-explicit bound

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\min_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\pi_{\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(n)}}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)\mid u,x_{\ell})\geq 1-C_{\ell}|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|\left[\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\log^{2}(e+n|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|)}{n^{1-\nu}}+\frac{(1+2\log(2/\delta))\log(en)}{B\,n^{m_{\nu}}}\right],`$ |  | (58) |

where $`C_{\ell}`$ is independent of $`n,B,\delta`$.

Let $`\Gamma`$ be one teacher path. Define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\omega_{\ell,u}:=\mathbb{E}[N_{u}(\Gamma)],\quad r_{\ell,u}(v):=\frac{\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]}{\omega_{\ell,u}},\qquad v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u).`$ |  | (59) |

Then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\omega_{\ell,u}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|},\quad r_{\ell,u}(v)>0.`$ |  | (60) |

See
[2](#Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2 (Single-task learning under SFT). ‣ SFT as off-policy learning ‣ 3.1 Single-task learnability ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")

#### Sketch of proof of Theorem [2](#Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2 (Single-task learning under SFT). ‣ SFT as off-policy learning ‣ 3.1 Single-task learnability ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

- •
  Derive the sampled SFT gradient and identify a convex potential whose
  gradient is its conditional expectation
  (Lemmas [12](#Thmlemma12 "Lemma 12 (Exact sampled SFT gradient and update support). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
  and [13](#Thmlemma13 "Lemma 13 (Convex potential, unbiased drift, and support error). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Construct a finite comparator with small potential and a controlled
  distance from the initial parameter
  (Lemma [14](#Thmlemma14 "Lemma 14 (A finite comparator approaching the optimal support). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Verify the conditional mini-batch noise and smoothness bounds,
  including the bounded initial transient
  (Lemmas [15](#Thmlemma15 "Lemma 15 (Conditional mini-batch noise). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
  and [16](#Thmlemma16 "Lemma 16 (Smoothness and a bounded initial transient). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Apply the finite-comparator last-iterate bound and evaluate the
  polynomial step-size sums
  (Lemmas [17](#Thmlemma17 "Lemma 17 (A finite-comparator last-iterate bound). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
  and [18](#Thmlemma18 "Lemma 18 (Polynomial step-size sums). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).
- •
  Convert the resulting potential bound into successor-set accuracy
  simultaneously at every teacher source
  (Lemmas [13](#Thmlemma13 "Lemma 13 (Convex potential, unbiased drift, and support error). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
  and [19](#Thmlemma19 "Lemma 19 (Simultaneous SFT successor-set learning). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")).

At iteration $`j`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{W})`$ | $`\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{t=0}^{T^{(b,j)}-1}\log\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(y_{t+1}^{*(b,j)}\mid y_{t}^{*(b,j)},x_{\ell}),`$ |  | (61) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{j}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}L_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j)}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}),`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j)}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}-\eta_{j}\mathbf{g}_{j}.`$ |  | (62) |

###### Lemma 12 (Exact sampled SFT gradient and update support).

The mini-batch update
Equation [62](#A2.E62 "In Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
has gradient
Equation [63](#A2.E63 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
whose Frobenius norm is at most $`G_{\ell}=\sqrt{2d(|V|-1)}`$. Every
update lies in $`\operatorname{span}\{x_{\ell}\}`$ within each source
block, and only sources in $`\mathcal{U}_{\ell}`$ can change.

###### Proof.

Direct differentiation gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{j}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}x_{\ell}\Biggl(N_{u}(\Gamma_{j}^{(b)})\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell})e_{u,v}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}N_{u,v}(\Gamma_{j}^{(b)})e_{u,v}\Biggr)^{\!\top}.`$ |  | (63) |

For one path $`\Gamma`$, let $`\mathbf{h}(\Gamma;\mathbf{W})`$ denote
the corresponding single-path gradient. If $`v_{\Gamma}(u)`$ is the
successor of the visited source $`u`$, then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell})e_{u,v}-e_{u,v_{\Gamma}(u)}\right\|_{2}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell})^{2}+1-2\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v_{\Gamma}(u)\mid u,x_{\ell})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq 2.`$ |  |

Since the source-node edge blocks are disjoint,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbf{h}(\Gamma;\mathbf{W})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 2\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}(|\Gamma|-1)\leq 2d(|V|-1).`$ |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}\leq G_{\ell}=\sqrt{2d(|V|-1)}.`$ |  | (64) |

∎

Define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)\mid u,x_{\ell}),`$ |  |

and

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\omega_{\ell,u}\left[D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(r_{\ell,u}\middle\|\frac{\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(\cdot\mid u,x_{\ell})}{P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})}\right)-\log P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})\right].`$ |  | (65) |

###### Lemma 13 (Convex potential, unbiased drift, and support error).

The potential $`F_{\ell}`$ in
Equation [65](#A2.E65 "In Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
is nonnegative and convex, the sampled gradient satisfies
Equation [67](#A2.E67 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
and
$`\max_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}[1-P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})]\leq|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})`$
for every finite $`\mathbf{W}`$.

###### Proof.

For every $`u`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(r_{\ell,u}\middle\|\frac{\pi_{\mathbf{W}}}{P_{\ell,u}}\right)-\log P_{\ell,u}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}r_{\ell,u}(v)\log r_{\ell,u}(v)-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}r_{\ell,u}(v)\log\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell}).`$ |  |

Therefore

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})=C_{\ell}^{\prime}-\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]\log\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell}),`$ |  | (66) |

so $`F_{\ell}`$ is convex. We next verify the conditional-drift identity
explicitly. Let $`\mathcal{F}_{j-1}`$ be the sigma-field generated by
all training randomness up to iteration $`j-1`$. Conditional on
$`\mathcal{F}_{j-1}`$, the parameter
$`\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}`$ is fixed, while
$`\Gamma_{j}^{(1)},\ldots,\Gamma_{j}^{(B)}`$ are conditionally
independent and each is uniformly distributed on $`\mathcal{P}_{\ell}`$.
Therefore, for every $`u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}`$,
$`v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)`$, and $`b\in\{1,\ldots,B\}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[N_{u}(\Gamma_{j}^{(b)})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[N_{u}(\Gamma)]=\omega_{\ell,u},`$ |  | $`\displaystyle\text{by conditional uniform sampling},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[N_{u,v}(\Gamma_{j}^{(b)})\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]=\omega_{\ell,u}r_{\ell,u}(v),`$ |  | $`\displaystyle\text{by Equation~\ref{eq:omega-r}}.`$ |  |

Moreover, for every correct teacher path $`\Gamma`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle N_{u}(\Gamma)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}N_{u,v}(\Gamma),`$ |  | $`\displaystyle\text{each source occurrence has one correct successor}.`$ |  |

Taking expectations gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\omega_{\ell,u}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)].`$ |  |

Now apply conditional expectation to
Equation [63](#A2.E63 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
Write $`\mathbf{W}_{j-1}:=\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}`$ only
within the following calculation. Since $`\mathbf{W}_{j-1}`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}_{j-1}`$-measurable, the softmax probabilities are fixed
under the conditional expectation. Substituting the two
conditional-expectation identities above into
Equation [63](#A2.E63 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\mathbf{g}_{j}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}x_{\ell}\Biggl(\omega_{\ell,u}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}_{j-1}}(z\mid u,x_{\ell})e_{u,z}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]e_{u,v}\Biggr)^{\!\top}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}x_{\ell}\Biggl(\omega_{\ell,u}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}_{j-1}}(z\mid u,x_{\ell})e_{u,z}-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]e_{u,v}\Biggr)^{\!\top}.`$ |  |

The second equality holds because the summand no longer depends on
$`b`$, so the factor $`B^{-1}`$ cancels with the sum over the $`B`$
mini-batch elements.

We next differentiate the potential.
Equation [66](#A2.E66 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}\log\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell}),`$ |  |

because $`C_{\ell}^{\prime}`$ is independent of $`\mathbf{W}`$. For the
softmax predictor,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle-\nabla_{\mathbf{W}}\log\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell})`$ | $`\displaystyle=x_{\ell}\left(\sum_{z\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(z\mid u,x_{\ell})e_{u,z}-e_{u,v}\right)^{\!\top}.`$ |  |

Substituting this identity and collecting the terms with the same source
$`u`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}x_{\ell}\Biggl(\omega_{\ell,u}\sum_{z\in\mathcal{N}(u)}\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(z\mid u,x_{\ell})e_{u,z}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt-\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]e_{u,v}\Biggr)^{\!\top}.`$ |  |

Here we used
$`\omega_{\ell,u}=\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}\mathbb{E}[N_{u,v}(\Gamma)]`$,
which follows from the preceding path-count identity. Evaluating this
identity at $`\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}`$ proves

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{g}_{j}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\nabla F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}).`$ |  | (67) |

Using

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\cdot\|\cdot)\geq 0,\quad-\log z\geq 1-z,`$ |  |

we obtain, for every $`u`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\omega_{\ell,u}[-\log P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})]\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}[1-P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})].`$ |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\max_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}[1-P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W})]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}).`$ |  | (68) |

∎

###### Lemma 14 (A finite comparator approaching the optimal support).

For every $`R>0`$, there is a finite $`\mathbf{W}_{R}`$ satisfying
$`F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{R})\leq C_{0}e^{-R}`$ and
$`\|\mathbf{W}_{R}-\mathbf{W}^{(0)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq C_{1}(1+R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|)^{2}`$,
with constants independent of $`R,n,B,\delta`$.

###### Proof.

For $`R>0`$, set

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle z_{u,v}^{(R)}=\begin{cases}R+\log r_{\ell,u}(v),&v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u),\\
0,&v\notin\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u).\end{cases}`$ |  | (69) |

Let

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle m_{\ell,u}^{-}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=|\mathcal{N}(u)\setminus\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)|.`$ |  |

Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}e^{z_{u,v}^{(R)}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=e^{R},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{v\notin\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u)}e^{z_{u,v}^{(R)}}`$ | $`\displaystyle=m_{\ell,u}^{-},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W}_{R})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{e^{R}}{e^{R}+m_{\ell,u}^{-}}=\frac{1}{1+m_{\ell,u}^{-}e^{-R}},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\pi_{\mathbf{W}_{R}}(v\mid u,x_{\ell})}{P_{\ell,u}(\mathbf{W}_{R})}`$ | $`\displaystyle=r_{\ell,u}(v),\qquad v\in\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(u).`$ |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{R})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\omega_{\ell,u}\log(1+m_{\ell,u}^{-}e^{-R})`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq e^{-R}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\omega_{\ell,u}m_{\ell,u}^{-}\leq|E|e^{-R}=:C_{0}e^{-R}.`$ |  | (70) |

Set

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle z_{u,v}^{(0)}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=x_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{(0)}e_{u,v},`$ |  |

and, for $`u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}`$, $`v\in\mathcal{N}(u)`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{R}e_{u,v}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\mathbf{W}^{(0)}e_{u,v}+\frac{z_{u,v}^{(R)}-z_{u,v}^{(0)}}{\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}}x_{\ell},`$ |  |

with all other columns unchanged. Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle x_{\ell}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{R}e_{u,v}`$ | $`\displaystyle=z_{u,v}^{(R)},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{R}-\mathbf{W}^{(0)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}}\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\sum_{v\in\mathcal{N}(u)}|z_{u,v}^{(R)}-z_{u,v}^{(0)}|^{2}.`$ |  |

Since

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 1,
``` |  |
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle r_{\ell,u}(v)\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad|z_{u,v}^{(R)}|\leq R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|,
``` |  |
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle Z_{\ell,0}:=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}\\
v\in\mathcal{N}(u)\end{subarray}}|z_{u,v}^{(0)}|<\infty,
``` |  |

we obtain

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{R}-\mathbf{W}^{(0)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq|E|(R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|+Z_{\ell,0})^{2}\leq C_{1}(1+R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|)^{2}.`$ |  | (71) |

∎

Write

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{j}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\mathbf{h}_{j}^{(b)},\quad\bm{\zeta}_{j}:=\mathbf{g}_{j}-\nabla F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}),`$ |  |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{h}_{j}^{(b)}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\mathbf{h}(\Gamma_{j}^{(b)};\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}),`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{j}^{(b)}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\mathbf{h}_{j}^{(b)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{h}_{j}^{(b)}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}].`$ |  |

Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\bm{\zeta}_{j}=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{(b)},\quad\mathbb{E}[\bm{\zeta}_{j}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]=0.`$ |  |

###### Lemma 15 (Conditional mini-batch noise).

The centered noise $`\bm{\zeta}_{j}`$ has conditional mean zero and
variance at most $`G_{\ell}^{2}/B`$. There is
$`\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}=O_{G}(B^{-1})`$ such that
Equation [73](#A2.E73 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
holds for $`0\leq\lambda\leq\sigma_{\ell,B}^{-2}`$.

###### Proof.

Conditional independence gives

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\zeta}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B^{2}}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\mathbb{E}[\|\bm{\xi}_{j}^{(b)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}]\leq\frac{G_{\ell}^{2}}{B}.`$ |  | (72) |

Let $`D:=d|E|`$. Since

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\bm{\xi}_{j}^{(b)}\|_{\text{F}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 2G_{\ell},`$ |  |

coordinate-wise Hoeffding gives, for every $`t>0`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\bm{\zeta}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}\geq t\mid\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right)`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 2D\exp\left(-\frac{Bt^{2}}{8DG_{\ell}^{2}}\right).`$ |  |

Hence, for a constant $`c_{D}>0`$ depending only on $`D`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}:=\frac{c_{D}G_{\ell}^{2}}{B},
``` |  |
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\|\bm{\zeta}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}{\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}}\right)\middle|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\leq e.
``` |  |

For $`0\leq\lambda\leq\sigma_{\ell,B}^{-2}`$, put
$`\theta:=\lambda\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}\in[0,1]`$. Then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\lambda\|\bm{\zeta}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(e^{\|\bm{\zeta}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}/\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}}\right)^{\theta}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\|\bm{\zeta}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}/\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}}\middle|\mathcal{F}_{j-1}\right]\right)^{\theta}`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq e^{\lambda\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}},`$ |  | (73) |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle=O_{G}(B^{-1}).`$ |  |

∎

###### Lemma 16 (Smoothness and a bounded initial transient).

The potential is $`L_{\ell}`$-smooth with $`L_{\ell}\leq d`$. For
$`0<\nu<1`$, there is a deterministic $`j_{0}`$, independent of
$`n,B,\delta`$, after which $`\eta_{j}\leq 1/(2L_{\ell})`$. The finite
comparator of
Lemma [14](#Thmlemma14 "Lemma 14 (A finite comparator approaching the optimal support). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
satisfies
Equation [75](#A2.E75 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
uniformly over the training history before $`j_{0}`$.

###### Proof.

For a perturbation $`H`$, let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle a_{u}(H):=x_{\ell}^{\top}He_{u,\cdot},\quad p_{u}:=\pi_{\mathbf{W}}(\cdot\mid u,x_{\ell}).`$ |  |

From
Equation ([66](#A2.E66 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")),

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla^{2}F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W})[H,H]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\omega_{\ell,u}a_{u}(H)^{\top}\left(\operatorname{Diag}(p_{u})-p_{u}p_{u}^{\top}\right)a_{u}(H)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ell}}\omega_{\ell,u}\|a_{u}(H)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\|H\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq d\|H\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.`$ |  |

Thus

|     |                            |                          |     |      |
|-----|----------------------------|--------------------------|-----|------|
|     | $`\displaystyle L_{\ell}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq d.`$ |     | (74) |

Since $`\eta_{j}=\Theta(j^{-\nu})`$, there exists a fixed $`j_{0}`$ such
that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\eta_{j}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2L_{\ell}},\qquad j\geq j_{0}.`$ |  |

For $`n<j_{0}`$,
Equation ([58](#A2.E58 "In B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"))
is made trivial by enlarging $`C_{\ell}`$. Hence assume $`n\geq j_{0}`$.
Also,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j_{0}-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\|_{\text{F}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{W}^{(0)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\|_{\text{F}}+G_{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{j_{0}-1}\eta_{j},`$ |  |

so

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j_{0}-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq C_{2}(1+R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|)^{2}.`$ |  | (75) |

∎

###### Lemma 17 (A finite-comparator last-iterate bound).

For every deterministic finite comparator $`\mathbf{W}_{R}`$ and
$`n\geq j_{0}`$, the bound
Equation [78](#A2.E78 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
holds with probability at least $`1-\delta`$. Its constants are
independent of $`n,B,\delta,R`$.

###### Proof.

We next verify explicitly that the last-iterate argument does not
require $`F_{\ell}`$ to attain its infimum at a finite parameter. Fix
the finite comparator $`\mathbf{W}_{R}`$ constructed above.
Equation [62](#A2.E62 "In Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 2\eta_{j}\left\langle\mathbf{g}_{j},\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\right\rangle=\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta_{j}^{2}\|\mathbf{g}_{j}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.`$ |  | (76) |

Moreover, convexity of $`F_{\ell}`$ and the definition of
$`\bm{\zeta}_{j}`$ imply

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)})-F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{R})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\nabla F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}),\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\right\rangle`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbf{g}_{j},\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\right\rangle-\left\langle\bm{\zeta}_{j},\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\right\rangle.`$ |  | (77) |

Equations [76](#A2.E76 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and
[77](#A2.E77 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
are the comparator-dependent one-step relations used in the Euclidean
convex specialization of Lemma 4.3 of [Liu and Zhou (2024)](#bib.bib17).
The subsequent weighted telescoping and martingale argument uses the
reference point only through these relations and the finite initial
Euclidean distance. Therefore, using
Equation [73](#A2.E73 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
the same argument applies with this arbitrary finite comparator
$`\mathbf{W}_{R}`$ and does not require a finite minimizer of
$`F_{\ell}`$.

Specializing that argument to

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\psi(\mathbf{W}):=\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},\quad\mu_{f}=\mu_{h}=M=0,\quad\gamma_{j}=\eta_{j},`$ |  |

thus gives, for every finite $`\mathbf{W}_{R}`$, with probability at
least $`1-\delta`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(n)})-F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{R})\leq C_{3}\Biggl[`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\|\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(j_{0}-1)}-\mathbf{W}_{R}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}{\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n}\eta_{j}}+\sigma_{\ell,B}^{2}\left(1+2\log\frac{2}{\delta}\right)\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n}\frac{\eta_{j}^{2}}{\sum_{s=j}^{n}\eta_{s}}\Biggr].`$ |  | (78) |

∎

###### Lemma 18 (Polynomial step-size sums).

For fixed $`0<\nu<1`$ and $`\eta_{j}=\Theta(j^{-\nu})`$,
$`\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n}\eta_{j}=\Theta(n^{1-\nu})`$ for sufficiently large
$`n`$ and the weighted noise sum satisfies
Equation [79](#A2.E79 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").

###### Proof.

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n}\eta_{j}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\Theta(n^{1-\nu}).`$ |  |

Moreover, with

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle S_{n}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{n}\frac{\eta_{j}^{2}}{\sum_{s=j}^{n}\eta_{s}}=S_{n}^{(1)}+S_{n}^{(2)},`$ |  |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle S_{n}^{(1)}`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\frac{\eta_{j}^{2}}{\sum_{s=j}^{n}\eta_{s}},\qquad S_{n}^{(2)}:=\sum_{j=\lfloor n/2\rfloor+1}^{n}\frac{\eta_{j}^{2}}{\sum_{s=j}^{n}\eta_{s}},`$ |  |

we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle S_{n}^{(1)}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{1-\nu}}\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}j^{-2\nu}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\begin{cases}O(n^{-\nu}),&0<\nu<1/2,\\
O(\log(en)n^{-1/2}),&\nu=1/2,\\
O(n^{-(1-\nu)}),&1/2<\nu<1,\end{cases}`$ |  |

and, for $`k=n-j+1`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle S_{n}^{(2)}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq Cn^{-\nu}\sum_{k=1}^{\lceil n/2\rceil}\frac{1}{k}=O(n^{-\nu}\log(en)).`$ |  |

Therefore

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle S_{n}`$ | $`\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{\log(en)}{n^{m_{\nu}}}\right).`$ |  | (79) |

∎

###### Lemma 19 (Simultaneous SFT successor-set learning).

For any finite initial parameter and fixed $`n\geq 1`$, the
batch-explicit bound
Equation [58](#A2.E58 "In B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
holds with probability at least $`1-\delta`$. No pretrained coverage
condition is needed for this assertion.

###### Proof.

Apply
Lemmas [14](#Thmlemma14 "Lemma 14 (A finite comparator approaching the optimal support). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")–[18](#Thmlemma18 "Lemma 18 (Polynomial step-size sums). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
Combining
Equations ([70](#A2.E70 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")),
([73](#A2.E73 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")),
([75](#A2.E75 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")),
([78](#A2.E78 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")),
and ([79](#A2.E79 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(n)})\leq C_{4}\left[e^{-R}+\frac{(1+R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|)^{2}}{n^{1-\nu}}+\frac{(1+2\log(2/\delta))\log(en)}{B\,n^{m_{\nu}}}\right].`$ |  |

Choose

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle R`$ | $`\displaystyle:=\log(e+n|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|).`$ |  | (80) |

Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\displaystyle e^{-R}=\frac{1}{e+n|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|}\leq\frac{1}{n},\qquad 1+R+\log|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|\leq 3\log(e+n|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|).
``` |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle F_{\ell}(\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{SFT}}^{(n)})\leq C_{5}\left[\frac{1}{n}+\frac{\log^{2}(e+n|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|)}{n^{1-\nu}}+\frac{(1+2\log(2/\delta))\log(en)}{B\,n^{m_{\nu}}}\right].`$ |  | (81) |

Lemma [13](#Thmlemma13 "Lemma 13 (Convex potential, unbiased drift, and support error). ‣ Sketch of proof of Theorem . ‣ B.5 Single-task learning under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
````
