repository](https://github.com/yuukiizayoi/continual-reasoning-experiments)
listed in the Reproducibility statement. The objective of this
experiment is to compare finite-mini-batch training with the retention
guarantees and population references associated with
Theorems [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3 (No forgetting under SD). ‣ Sustainability of SD ‣ 3.2 Continual learning and catastrophic forgetting ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [4](#Thmtheorem4 "Theorem 4 (Catastrophic forgetting under SFT). ‣ Forgetting in SFT ‣ 3.2 Continual learning and catastrophic forgetting ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
Concrete numerical settings are listed below.

### C.1 Experiment settings

#### Graph construction and initialization

We instantiate the layered DAG constructed in
Appendix [B.1](#A2.SS1 "B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
The graph parameters and the choices used to construct
$`\mathbf{W}^{(0)}`$ are summarized separately in
Tables [2](#A3.T2 "Table 2 ‣ Graph construction and initialization ‣ C.1 Experiment settings ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [2](#A3.T2 "Table 2 ‣ Graph construction and initialization ‣ C.1 Experiment settings ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning").
All tasks use the same graph and all seeds use the same deterministic
initialization.

|  Parameter                                    |  Value            |
|-----------------------------------------------|-------------------|
|  Tasks $`L`$                                  |  $`4`$            |
|  Clusters $`K`$                               |  $`13`$           |
|  Node layers $`(H+1)`$                        |  $`5`$            |
|  Nodes $`|V|`$                                |  $`123`$          |
|  Directed edges $`|E|`$                       |  $`214`$          |
|  Correct paths $`|\mathcal{P}_{\ell}|`$       |  $`10`$ per task  |
|  Maximum valid boundaries $`M`$               |  $`10`$           |
|  Boundary nodes $`\sum_{k}|\partial C_{k}|`$  |  $`53`$           |

Table 1: Graph parameters.

|  Parameter                                    |  Value                 |
|-----------------------------------------------|------------------------|
|  Routing error parameter $`\epsilon_{1}`$     |  $`0.02`$              |
|  Inter-cluster probability $`\epsilon_{2}`$   |  $`0.04`$              |
|  Input dimension $`d`$                        |  $`36`$                |
|  Task-specific input coordinates              |  $`4`$-D one-hot       |
|  Input squared norm $`\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}`$  |  $`33`$                |
|  Residual successors $`|R_{k}(u)|`$           |  $`1`$                 |
|  Each start-to-entry probability              |  $`1/10`$              |
|  Boundary residual mass                       |  $`0.92`$ or $`0.88`$  |

Table 2: Initialization parameters.

At a task start, $`|\partial_{\ell}^{\star}C_{k}|=10`$ and
$`I_{\ell,k}(s)=\varnothing`$, so
Equation [29](#A2.E29 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Proposition . ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
assigns probability $`1/10`$ to each entry boundary. At a relevant
shared router, $`|\partial_{\ell}^{\star}C_{k}|=1`$ and the remaining
successors form $`I_{\ell,k}(s)`$; the correct exit receives probability
$`1/(1+\epsilon_{1})`$ and each other exit receives
$`\epsilon_{1}/((1+\epsilon_{1})|I_{\ell,k}(s)|)`$. For task inputs not
using that router, its outgoing distribution is uniform. At every
boundary,
Equation [33](#A2.E33 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Proposition . ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
assigns probability $`\epsilon_{2}`$ to each inter-cluster edge and
probability $`1-d_{u,k}^{\mathrm{out}}\epsilon_{2}`$ to its unique
same-cluster successor. There are $`51`$ boundaries with inter-cluster
outdegree $`2`$ and $`2`$ with outdegree $`3`$. With $`K=13`$ and
$`\epsilon_{2}=0.04`$, the sufficient condition in
Appendix [B.1](#A2.SS1 "B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
holds since $`(K-1)\epsilon_{2}=0.48<1/2`$.

The input matrix is $`X=[\mathbf{1}_{4\times 32}\;\;I_{4}]`$, where the
first block has all entries equal to one. Thus $`XX^{\top}`$ has
diagonal entries $`33`$ and off-diagonal entries $`32`$ and is
invertible. We use
Equation [35](#A2.E35 "In Proof. ‣ Sketch of proof of Proposition . ‣ B.1 Realizability on routing-compatible layered DAGs ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
directly:

|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{(0)}e_{u,v}`$ | $`\displaystyle=X^{\top}(XX^{\top})^{-1}\ell_{u,v},`$ | $`\displaystyle\ell_{u,v}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\bigl(\log P_{1}(v\mid u),\ldots,\log P_{4}(v\mid u)\bigr)^{\top}.`$ |  | (130) |

The hint parameter $`\epsilon_{\mathrm{rel}}=0.1`$ is used during SD
training, not to construct $`\mathbf{W}^{(0)}`$.

#### Pairwise and cumulative conflict rates.

Tables [4](#A3.T4 "Table 4 ‣ Pairwise and cumulative conflict rates. ‣ C.1 Experiment settings ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [4](#A3.T4 "Table 4 ‣ Pairwise and cumulative conflict rates. ‣ C.1 Experiment settings ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
report conflict rates, with rows denoting evaluated old tasks and
columns denoting the tasks being learned. The pairwise rate $`\theta`$
compares only the row task and the column task. The cumulative rate
$`\theta_{\mathrm{total}}`$ is the fraction of the row task’s correct
paths that conflict with at least one task learned after it, up to and
including the column task. Thus, $`\theta_{\mathrm{total}}`$ is computed
from a union of conflict-path sets: overlapping paths are counted only
once, and the pairwise rates are not added. Diagonal entries are set to
zero; entries below the diagonal are omitted.

| Old / New | Task 1 | Task 2  | Task 3  | Task 4  |
|-----------|--------|---------|---------|---------|
| Task 1    | $`0`$  | $`0.4`$ | $`0.6`$ | $`0.6`$ |
| Task 2    |        | $`0`$   | $`0.9`$ | $`0.9`$ |
| Task 3    |        |         | $`0`$   | $`0.7`$ |
| Task 4    |        |         |         | $`0`$   |

Table 3: Pairwise conflict rate $`\theta`$.

| Old / New | Task 1 | Task 2  | Task 3  | Task 4  |
|-----------|--------|---------|---------|---------|
| Task 1    | $`0`$  | $`0.4`$ | $`0.7`$ | $`1.0`$ |
| Task 2    |        | $`0`$   | $`0.9`$ | $`0.9`$ |
| Task 3    |        |         | $`0`$   | $`0.7`$ |
| Task 4    |        |         |         | $`0`$   |

Table 4: Cumulative conflict rate $`\theta_{\mathrm{total}}`$.

For example, Task 1 has pairwise conflict rates $`0.4`$ and $`0.6`$ with
Tasks 2 and 3, respectively, whereas their cumulative conflict rate is
$`0.7`$. In the plots, the term labeled “Limit” uses
$`\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{old}}(1-\theta_{\mathrm{total}})`$, with the
concentration profile from
Equation [89](#A2.E89 "In B.7 Catastrophic forgetting under SFT ‣ Appendix B Realizability and proofs ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
constructed from the old task’s correct-path probabilities at the end of
its own training. This profile is held fixed throughout subsequent-task
training, while $`\theta_{\mathrm{total}}`$ is updated to reflect the
cumulative conflict. Thus the plotted term uses the cumulative rate,
rather than the pairwise $`\theta`$, and is computed separately for each
seed before averaging. The label refers to this concentration-profile
term, not necessarily to the exact limiting correct generation
probability.

#### Training and evaluation

We train four tasks sequentially with $`2\times 10^{6}`$ gradient
updates per task, using mini-batches of size $`B=32`$ and seeds
$`0,\ldots,9`$ for each method. The step size is
$`\eta_{j}=11^{-1}j^{-0.01}`$, where $`j`$ starts at $`1`$ for each
task. The factor $`11^{-1}=3/\|x_{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}`$ controls the update
magnitude, while $`j^{-0.01}`$ provides a slow decay over successive
updates. Plots show the mean correct generation probability and the
minimum–maximum range across the ten seeds. Each probability is
evaluated by summing the probabilities of all ten correct paths. For SD,
the graph-specific population learning lower bound is shown during
own-task training. At its end, the plot switches to the conditional
retention lower bound anchored at the observed endpoint for each seed.
The population learning curve is evaluated at $`B\to\infty`$ for the
stability.

#### Graph-specific evaluation of constants.

We tighten intermediate bounds in the Appendix proofs using the
experimental graph, without altering the theorem statements. For
Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3 (No forgetting under SD). ‣ Sustainability of SD ‣ 3.2 Continual learning and catastrophic forgetting ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
we use edge-specific hint and visitation probabilities in the odds bound
and sum correct-path probabilities instead of applying the coarse
coupling union bound. For
Theorem [4](#Thmtheorem4 "Theorem 4 (Catastrophic forgetting under SFT). ‣ Forgetting in SFT ‣ 3.2 Continual learning and catastrophic forgetting ‣ 3 Main results ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning"),
we evaluate the source-wise optimization and cross-task
probability-transfer bounds using the actual visitation frequencies,
logit offsets, and unchanged path factors. These refinements retain the
underlying inequalities while avoiding worst-case estimates over the
general graph family. The SD learning and SFT upper reference curves are
evaluated in the population limit $`B\to\infty`$. The SFT conditional
lower and SD retention bounds are instead evaluated at the observed
phase endpoints, separately for each seed.

### C.2 Experiment results

Table [5](#A3.T5 "Table 5 ‣ C.2 Experiment results ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
reports the correct-generation probabilities after all four tasks have
been learned. SFT reaches a mean probability above $`0.999997`$ at the
end of each task’s own training, but its final means on Tasks 1–3 fall
to $`0.002799`$, $`0.100312`$, and $`0.304232`$, respectively. SD
attains approximately $`0.980386`$ on every task and preserves its
own-training endpoint through the final task for every seed, to stored
numerical precision. The final seed-to-seed range for SD is below
$`1.2\times 10^{-8}`$ for each task.

| Task | SFT | SD |
|----|----|----|
| Task 1 | $`0.002799\;[0.002701,\,0.002955]`$ | $`0.980386\;[0.980386,\,0.980386]`$ |
| Task 2 | $`0.100312\;[0.065468,\,0.149365]`$ | $`0.980386\;[0.980386,\,0.980386]`$ |
| Task 3 | $`0.304232\;[0.263488,\,0.334231]`$ | $`0.980386\;[0.980386,\,0.980386]`$ |
| Task 4 | $`0.999998\;[0.999998,\,0.999998]`$ | $`0.980386\;[0.980386,\,0.980386]`$ |

Table 5: Final correct-generation probabilities after all four tasks.
Entries are means \[minimum, maximum\] over ten seeds, rounded to six
decimal places.

All saved evaluations satisfy the applicable SFT conditional lower and
SD retention bounds, within numerical tolerance. The SFT trajectories
also remain below the population upper references at the saved
evaluations. For SD, $`12`$ of $`12{,}400`$ saved learning evaluations
lie below the population learning reference, with a maximum shortfall of
$`7.83\times 10^{-6}`$. This distinction is consistent with using
$`B=32`$ for training: the $`B\to\infty`$ learning reference does not
guarantee finite-mini-batch accuracy.

Figures [3](#A3.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ C.2 Experiment results ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
and [4](#A3.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ C.2 Experiment results ‣ Appendix C Numerical experiment ‣ Learning without Overwriting: A Theory of Self-Distillation and Supervised Fine-Tuning in Continual Reasoning")
show the training dynamics for SD and SFT, respectively.

Figure 3: Training dynamics under SD for Tasks 1–4. The population
learning lower reference is plotted during own-task training. At its
end, the conditional retention lower bound is anchored at the observed
endpoint and applies throughout subsequent-task training.

Figure 4: Training dynamics under SFT for Tasks 1–4. Black curves show
conditional retention lower bounds and upper references. The
concentration-profile term
$`\mathfrak{C}_{\mathrm{old}}(1-\theta_{\mathrm{total}})`$ reflects the
cumulative conflict and, for Task 1, the accumulation of forgetting
across subsequent tasks.
